# Task-23 attempt 2 — pre-registration invalid-endpoint exclusions

**Status: corrected; no endpoint repair is required for the cases below.** The operator decision recorded at 2026-11-10 00:57:42 UTC classifies pre-registration `ENOTSOCK` and `ENOTDIR` on `ARC_SOCKET_PATH` as invalid endpoint configuration and requires no child launch. The earlier attempt incorrectly classified these as outages. Its reproduction and failed launch expectation are retained below. The existing corrected-source gate collection remains the evidence for the backend gates; independent QA and the Chief's final decision remain downstream. Skill used: `oma-backend`.

## Native reproduction

On Darwin 27.2.0 arm64, `python3 target/exec-fail-open/task23-attempt2-reproduction-b34c85c3-beb3-421c-83b9-42f52f9e735c/reproduce.py` exited 1 because it expected launches for invalid endpoint configurations. That expectation conflicts with the subsequent operator decision; the nonzero exit remains a recorded failed probe expectation. For each replacement case the probe bound a real Unix socket, closed it and replaced either the socket entry with a regular file or the parent directory with a regular file before starting `arcctl exec`. It used the native `arc-exec-fixture` in `echo` mode with an invocation marker and checked exit 17 and one marker line.

| Case | Native connect errno | Wrapper exit | Child invocations | Diagnostic |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- |
| Absent socket control | 2 (ENOENT) | 17 | 1 | `TRACKING_UNAVAILABLE` |
| Socket replaced by file | 38 (ENOTSOCK) | 1 | 0 | `NOT_STARTED` |
| Socket parent replaced by file | 20 (ENOTDIR) | 1 | 0 | `NOT_STARTED` |

The two invalid-endpoint cases reported `Local execution preparation failed. No child was started.` The measured parent-to-wrapper-exit intervals were 8.160 ms and 7.883 ms; these are not child-handshake or pre-spawn deadline measurements. Recovery and replay were not tested in this reproduction. The source fingerprint before and after was `bd81c863144d1dd2b149122fa47299c60f503064eda68262fa1d464c08212fdb` across 242 inputs. The executable hashes were `80410fb9bf679162e27a410d723dc6aecfd37d063e5fe6340685b9784eaa9f1b` for `target/debug/arcctl` and `7ff797bbb98c7e69c6cc093f4f94d5526bf22161928091c07709ab9a9db36185` for the child fixture. The probe, full per-case outputs, process identifiers and timestamps are retained under the reproduction directory; `reproduction.json` SHA-256 is `c9e75722363ee74cd7c1d81356c7e1b32538f85a70c540ba7a3870d3c0e00281`, and `reproduce.py` SHA-256 is `43b7f0d757ea6c042f576a930eb808a1cff1f386e28faaf4a44aeb6a6b12eadb`.

`CARGO_NET_OFFLINE=true cargo test --workspace invalid_socket -- --nocapture --test-threads=1` exited 0: four matching tests passed, including two native tests that require the fail-closed behavior above; other tests were filtered. It took 1.937 seconds, with identical 242-input fingerprints before and after. `test-receipt.json`, `test.stdout`, and `test.stderr` are in the same reproduction directory; their SHA-256 values are `96c58f05fd8abd93607096507c5ffc6360e99ec9358b7f13e661203b4e3c0bb6`, `68384a467b5887744de098767b0d41fe2f6ad8426e1642060f6c80d67a89d758`, and `eeec48b4b667a43e3821c377258349e5082a48a809f302de9eb38620912ac62e`. This focused run is not the required unfiltered workspace gate.

`crates/arc-cli/src/exec/transport.rs` classifies ENOTSOCK and ENOTDIR as `InvalidEndpoint`, while `crates/arc-cli/src/exec/preparation.rs` propagates that result during REGISTER. The live assertions in `crates/arc-daemon/tests/managed_exec/invalid_socket.rs` and `crates/arc-cli/src/exec/transport/access_tests.rs` enforce the operator-approved non-launching behavior. The absent-socket control instead shows the fail-open connection-outage behavior. Lost acknowledgements after a sent request remain subject to the uncertain-tracking behavior covered by the corrected-source collection below. No product change or gate recollection is required for these pre-registration invalid-endpoint observations.

Framework independent review must inspect this correction, verify the original bytes and source transition, reproduce the unfiltered docs/compact/compact-flow checks, and assess the refreshed gate. The downstream `final.reviewTransition` can be the exact object from `target/exec-fail-open/task13-preserved/source-transition.json`. The current validator also requires a separate `final.correctionReview`: a current `context(root)` receipt with `kind: correction-review`, `actorId: reviewer`, `skill: oma-qa`, `status: passed`, zero blockers/findings, the actual framework review UUID, the refreshed `gates` reference, `transitionSha256` equal to SHA-256 of `JSON.stringify(final.reviewTransition)`, a `reviewedAt` timestamp after the checked observations, and current-source reviewer command receipts for the three unfiltered checks. This is an interface for the later independent reviewer and Chief; no such review or receipt is asserted here. The Chief must then refresh final and terminal artifacts and run the remaining terminal evidence check without changing the 11+2 partition.
Desktop integration, native Windows acceptance, vendor hooks and scheduling remain outstanding.

### Interface and task-4 instructions

A `combined` container contains explicitly scoped `current` and `archive` entries. Its outer `path` and SHA-256 bind the exact regular staged container bytes; each selector also binds the original logical path and entry SHA-256. Current selection rejects archive entries, and archive extraction rejects current entries. Tagged entries cannot be relabeled by changing a combined envelope into a legacy container. Duplicate/unsafe paths, altered bytes or identity, unstaged/index-mismatched bytes and nonregular paths remain rejected. Existing untagged collection and archive references remain usable. Unselected entries are checked for integrity but not followed as semantic dependencies. The same successor and entry-aware cycle guards, source/host and Actor/skill checks, command partition, canonical paths and contract graph remain in force.

After task-10 is integrated, task-4 must run the unchanged eleven prerequisite commands and capture their real outputs and native observations into a fresh pending directory under `target/exec-fail-open/`. With all four current canonical files still present at their original paths, seal exactly one new file (the example name below is available in this snapshot):

```sh
bun scripts/harness/exec-fail-open-collection.ts --combined target/exec-fail-open/task4-refresh/pending .arc-verify/exec-fail-open/implementation/collection-next.json
git add -- .arc-verify/exec-fail-open/implementation/collection-next.json
```

The maintained collector returns the container reference and entry references; save its actual `{path, sha256}` result. The container does not yet exist and this report invents no digest. Immediately after staging, before assembly, use `canonicalArchive(root, reference(root, newPath))` and `archiveBytes(root, refs[canonicalPath])` to verify all four returned buffers equal `canonicalBytes(root, canonicalPath)`. Use `compactReferences(root, reference(root, oldPath))` and `artifact(root, ref)` for each returned reference to verify the untouched prior collection; also check its SHA-256 above. A combined current selector resolves through `artifact`/`boundJSON`; an archive selector resolves only through `archiveBytes`. The old archive and old collection are retained at their original paths.

Then pass the same new container as the current collection and the predecessor archive to the existing assembler:

```sh
bun scripts/harness/exec-fail-open-assemble.ts --compact .arc-verify/exec-fail-open/implementation/collection-next.json .arc-verify/exec-fail-open/implementation/collection-next.json fmt clippy workspace coverage harness preservation clean-checkout coverage-cli coverage-harness live-coverage docs
```

For each canonical replacement the assembler verifies the matching archived bytes and staged container first. If later validation fails it restores the captured originals. After successful replacement, exact preceding bytes remain retrievable with `archiveBytes`; a deliberate restoration can write those returned buffers to their matching canonical paths. Do not overwrite the new immutable container or prior collection. Continue the existing task-4 process for source-bound gates, independent QA and terminal evidence. The four production required-test suffixes were added to the existing inventory; no execution or existence of task-10 tests is claimed here.

Serena was absent from the available tool inventory; known assigned paths were read directly. No Rust, contracts, preservation pins, migration, accounting files, manifests or lockfiles were edited.

The accepted committed baseline remains `00773c69a8e5e7fd417fd5cc78d03e222364f609`, tree `b98f996caa04fe13eeeeab1072c3c528f6d18c65`. This actor's isolated input snapshot and local HEAD are `191a547482ead36f6e6b90f71eaf8a513a8222af`, tree `87bb65a6fcc6eabf183d8e12397ad2743699d5d7`; the accepted baseline does not contain the actor snapshot's uncommitted or snapshot-only additions. Before editing, the current-input fingerprint was `01c53b06d261d93ec45b6d8247c83e80b82692f5f5759b0fce1d9ae0e27c5cde` across 224 files, matching the Chief's recorded input; the baseline-relative product-scope diff SHA-256 was `d060160e4c276c28532c5aab077ba0b5adeba612c247a7e718e124df8425e2c3`. After the changes, the actor-local working-input fingerprint is `702760b87a8aaa4b5bceca47654494c3c99ba1420f91b5082bf34b76a37c3221` across 224 files, and the corresponding baseline-relative diff SHA-256 is `c23c04e1cb7ccb16291f10dd2d960df4f00ec27f46b5f3e155cab3fed5968060`. These are not claims about a later integrated checkout.

The shared guard resolves successor references against the active validation root and the traversal rejects noncanonical evidence paths before reading. Nested `path` and selector fields cannot hide the five successor artifacts via leading `./`, active-root absolute paths, repeated separators, dot components or parent components. Terminal validator paths in command fields remain rejected. Unrelated paths and plain non-reference descriptions remain usable. Traversal now attempts to parse every reachable selected or legacy record regardless of logical extension; malformed required JSON still fails, opaque non-JSON remains usable, and only selected records and reachable predecessors are inspected. Whole containers remain integrity-checked without making unselected siblings or archive entries semantic prerequisites. Selected content cannot evade inspection by claiming the container kind.

The two original defects were reproduced before the fix through `validateGateData` in an otherwise passing isolated fixture. For both successor presence states and both legacy and selected storage, a canonical `qa/review.json` reference was rejected while its `./` and active-root absolute aliases were accepted. A selected, staged `boundJSON` receipt containing the canonical successor was rejected with a `.json` logical name but accepted with `.txt` or no extension. Each negative had a clean counterpart. The exact test-only RED patch is `target/exec-fail-open/task11-attempt7/red.patch`, SHA-256 `3befb4d8bcca6259f3dccac8d0387a4a6ef6374a2118619a9132798b1110afe2`; its input fingerprint was `312fcb46fe43d57569a4234c83e17285a6dfae48f00be4d2cacb1d31b6e3f4b8`.

The final complete-gate fixtures cover ordinary and staged selected receipts, all five actual successor paths, aliases nested in path and selector fields, successors absent and present, `.json`, `.txt` and extensionless JSON, both terminal validator commands, unrelated names and executable paths, opaque non-JSON, malformed `.json`, staged-byte tampering, entry-aware cycles, two selected siblings in one container and an unselected successor-bearing sibling. The existing 140-file and 143-file compact flows, archive round trips, immutable overwrite, duplicate/unsafe selector, stale/failed receipt, Actor/skill identity and root-isolation checks remain in place.

### Blocking interface conflict for Chief coordination

The unchanged required `bun test scripts/coverage/clean-checkout.test.ts` invokes the exact verification-contract command `bun test scripts/coverage/evidence.test.ts` at `scripts/coverage/clean-checkout.test.ts:45-47` through `command(..., 3600000)`. `scripts/coverage/commands.ts:8-10` starts that child and sends it SIGTERM at the specified timer. The current assignment explicitly requires every replay or wrapper of a verification-contract command to allow at least 7,200,000 ms. Therefore running this unmodified clean-checkout test would launch the nested contract command under a one-hour killing wrapper, despite the compliant two-hour outer receipt. Both source files are byte-identical to accepted HEAD and protected by `scripts/coverage/current-preservation.ts`; editing tests or validators is outside this assignment. The Chief must coordinate this concrete mismatch with the coverage/harness owner while preserving the original checks and accounting protections. No operator choice of repair method is required.

A bounded in-process probe executed the real `command()` implementation with a stubbed `Bun.spawn` and intercepted timer: exact argv `bun test scripts/coverage/evidence.test.ts`, observed timer `3600000` ms, required minimum `7200000` ms. It launched no child and is fixture-only, not a passing product check. ## Task-19 attempt 2: admission and retained evidence

**Repair scope:** record the live mission budgets and preserve the existing task-19 gate and observations. Skill: `oma-backend`. The live, read-only mission record was obtained from the mission controller's persisted state, not inferred from the supplied historical operation summary. No mission state was modified.
